## Current continuation — 18 September 2026, Australia/Sydney

Latest checkpoint, **11:55 AEST**: v94 is retained as a non-authorising
historical prefix after a WSL/Docker restart. Its no-cache three-role build and
pinned-CDP probe passed, and its immutable browser checkpoint remains at
**63/110** passed vectors with 64 recorded results: vector 34's first attempt
was an operational failure, its unchanged retry passed, and vector 64 has an
outstanding intent from the host interruption. Supported lifecycle recovery
removed the five exited containers and one empty network without changing the
checkpoint. An unchanged-source resume then failed closed before checkpoint
mutation because Docker reported `MemTotal` 8,192 bytes lower after reboot
(`16,508,788,736` to `16,508,780,544`), while all other daemon fields,
including the server UUID, matched exactly.

That failure exposed an overbound execution contract rather than a scientific
or defence failure. Reviewed Lab commit
`9aed576a13659a51b0c25c6f81bf9115ebb8e149` introduces browser-egress
foundation schema 6: daemon identity/configuration remains exact, 12 CPUs are
still mandatory, and positive raw `MemTotal` is recorded in the foundation and
each runtime result as provenance without an invented threshold. Foundation
schema 5 is now portable historical evidence and retains its original exact
daemon, v2-manifest and packet/DNS replay semantics. Focused schema/runtime,
authority/watcher, CLI and fresh-result suites passed 32, 65, 66 and 15 tests;
the synthetic 110-result append/replay/final/deep-verification chain also
passed in 954.30 seconds. Rust/Gitlink remains clean at
`46313bef90ad392b7ca293ab7cf28108d2f35c7f`. The allocator chain is dense
through consumed v94, every v95 destination is absent, and **v95 is the exact
next fresh cohort**. Its no-cache build has not yet launched. Pilot 0/120,
final classes 0/100, compatibility 0/900 and formal 0/16,000 remain unchanged.

frozen in clean Lab commit

remain `46313bef90ad392b7ca293ab7cf28108d2f35c7f`. Supported lifecycle

Evidence audit: 2026-09-11, Australia/Sydney (AEST, UTC+10)

This is the current, thesis-oriented ledger for the QCSD workspace. It records
what is implemented, what has been executed and sealed, what may be claimed,
and what remains before BuFLO and CS-BuFLO can be promoted. Detailed debugging
chronology is retained separately as local archival context; it is explanatory
only and is not an operational or evidentiary authority.

<a id="source-project-md-l353"></a>
## 1. Document contract and current status

<a id="source-project-md-l355"></a>
### 1.1 Source-of-truth order

When two descriptions disagree, use this order:

1. the current Git checkout for implementation behaviour;
2. checked-in specifications, parameter receipts, campaign manifests, and
   primary-reference receipts;
3. immutable receipts and checksum-closed outputs from an executed cohort;
4. explanatory prose in this ledger and the Lab README.

An implementation checkout describes present code. An executed receipt proves
only the exact source, image, parameters, and inputs to which it is bound. A
successful older receipt cannot authorise newer source.

Exact v83 Lab `ea4cf2b65b4a3fd650c0497beda600ea235db456` binds the same clean
Rust/gitlink and carries observer implementation commit
`b466cd63e8f17835a75e10e97718cf4aae8b304e`, which repairs that evidence
boundary. Packet-analysis schema 4 uses
strictly increasing `frame.number` as file/capture order, preserves raw
timestamps, and receipts the count and maximum size of adjacent timestamp
regressions as non-policy diagnostics. Historical packet-analysis schema 3
retains its original timestamp-monotonic rule during replay. A two-phase
observer now closes and hashes the PCAP, publishes capture-closure schema 1,
lets the host create and fsync an exact hash/size-bound copy, and only then
begins parsing after an explicit acknowledgement. If parsing fails, the closure
and PCAP are bound only as non-promoted failure evidence. Passing capture
receipts remain schema 2. Independent review found no open blocker; the settled
observer/qualification/launcher suite passed 499/499 in 856.64 seconds, with
focused observer and browser-launcher runs passing 34/34 and 56/56.

Because v83 reached a source defect after its immutable 76-vector prefix, it

blocked-Document failure, a later same-ID error-document finish, and three

browser-egress, 0/900 compatibility certification, and 0/16,000 formal capture.
The prospective 100-class endpoint remains governed by the checked-in
machine-readable class-study contract and fail-closed coordinator; no

The immediately following v69 browser-egress `create` crossed the previously
failing supervisor boundary, published its foundation and resumable checkpoint,
and launched the first genuine live vector,
`constructor--page--websocket`. Attempt 1 ended as an operational failure when
the 115-second role watchdog expired during the browser-action stage. The
checkpoint remains `running`, with `next_vector_ordinal=1`, no passed vectors,
and two historical retries still available. There is no final browser-egress
receipt and no scientific sample; v69 is an open historical diagnostic lineage,
not authority for a repaired checkout and not a failed scientific campaign.

Exact-image diagnosis found a Playwright 1.57 synchronous re-entry deadlock:
calling public `WebSocketRoute.close()` inside its synchronous route callback
blocked the callback thread. Current client-side source queues the same close
through the pinned private channel's no-reply path and fails closed if that
exact adapter shape is unavailable. The fixture now requires the browser to
observe close code `1008`, reason `QCSD non-replayable egress policy`, and
`wasClean=true`; a merely issued action cannot pass. It also resolves resumed
build authority relative to the fixed Lab root, not the caller's working
directory, and raises per-role and supervisor bounds to 300 and 315 seconds.
A manual run with current source bind-mounted into the exact v69 preparation
image completed the repaired action in about 0.585 seconds, while a separate
observation saw the exact close tuple in about 0.75 seconds. These are
diagnostic engineering proofs only, not an immutable 110-vector receipt.

Because that repair postdates every v69 image and receipt, v69 must not be
resumed to authorise current source. The repair is committed at clean Lab
`03b0953235e45dcbd71cfcad63b3342924f9c3b8` with unchanged clean Rust/gitlink
`ce0d7a21756ce795d6f50d750a1c25e0fa006327`.

left `next_vector_ordinal=1`. The live Docker 29 topology had exactly the four
required direct members—browser, fixture, forbidden sink, and DNS sink—while
the observer correctly shared the browser network namespace. The launcher
performed its membership projection only after role shutdown, by which point
Docker had cleared `.Containers`, endpoint IDs, and IP addresses. This is a
temporal runtime-projection defect, not stale Docker state, a defence failure,
or an accepted vector.

Clean Lab `60909195e3138957b8fdd12d70dedbf4eeb03224` implements the
two-phase schema-3 projection: it binds live network membership before role
shutdown, then binds stopped-container state and teardown separately. It also
requires complete, correctly typed raw inspect fields and cross-checks the
immutable container identity and configuration across both snapshots.
Engineering validation passed all 444 affected tests, including a real Docker
29 lifecycle test that proves the observer and browser share one Linux network
namespace, plus the complete synthetic 110-vector closure; the remaining Lab
suite passed 4,049 tests with 17 declared skips. The total is 4,493 passed, 17
skipped and no failures. Bash syntax, diff whitespace and fatal Ruff checks
also pass. This is clean implementation evidence but not source-bound build or
qualification evidence by itself, so it could not inherit v77 authority.

The producer merged privilege-dependent stderr and stdout for both version
queries. Deep verification first rejected the resulting `tshark` packet-decoder
binding; the `dumpcap` capture-tool binding had the same latent defect. The
launcher therefore misclassified a successful capture. The sealed
attempt/result is immutable, non-authorising Lab-orchestration failure evidence;
the open v78 checkpoint will not be reused. This is not a Docker,
browser-preparation, workload, client, or defence failure and advances no

The implementation descends from the browser-egress and extended-foundation

Lab `e719219b42826126fdc7771c2dec0463d425a83d` historically separated the Docker
client and Buildx configuration lifetimes under the lifecycle guardian. The
launcher and guardian required the same non-root UID/GID, required
`CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` to be absent from every capability set, exposed an empty
anonymous mode-`0500` `DOCKER_CONFIG`, and retained a separately linked
mode-`0700` `BUILDX_CONFIG`. That committed mode-`0500` boundary intended
optional token-seed writes during trusted, pinned, credentialless
BuildKit/Buildx pulls to use their upstream in-memory fallback; v61 subsequently
proved that its pathname lifetime was insufficient during pinned-frontend
resolution. The exact historical affected suite passed 476 tests with eight
explicit skips. A separate historical opt-in live metadata probe passed 1/1 and
observed Docker's selected Buildx plugin at
`/usr/local/lib/docker/cli-plugins/docker-buildx`, version
`v0.29.1-desktop.1`, commit `28f6246ff24e2c05095e8741e48c48dcb2d3b4bc`,
and resolved-target SHA-256
`ceabe0401307ae5db2fe2ec9eff90959db0638a0dbcce52320bde2be20104347`.

The committed post-v61 repair instead keeps the empty Docker configuration
pathname-linked at mode `0500` with link count two, as a non-symlink sibling of
the lifecycle lock. Its name binds the invoking UID, guardian start time, and a
fresh nonce; cleanup removes the exact pathname only after supervised users
drain, while a published mode-`0500` crash residue requires a conservative
process-reference census before removal. V67 proved this build boundary and
the post-v64 directory-permission repair with a completed source-bound
execution/completion pair for exact Lab `93f7cfc0…` / Rust `ce0d7a21…`.
Its pinned-CDP probe nevertheless failed before receipt, and the post-v67
CDP/worker repair has no build pair or scientific authority. The implementation uses
build-execution schema 5 and the separate build-completion schema-1 success
boundary described later in this subsection, together with the corresponding
downstream identity bumps.

Every container-launching `class-study` action now runs the exact checkout's
host-only `qcsd_lab.class_build_admission` resolver under `/usr/bin/python3 -I`
before Docker access or stale-state mutation. The resolver reopens every direct
and transitive authority carrier consumed by the action and requires one clean
source, one current schema-5 execution/schema-1 completion pair, and one set of
collection, preparation, and reference image IDs. Base, non-successor cohort
and campaign actions require the current acquisition-completion carrier;
`fit-numeric` and `prefix-specs` require the exact frozen `--capture-result`
before Docker. Frozen resume inputs and immutable successor plans are
reconstructed rather than trusted by path.

The durable allocator now distinguishes an opened directory entry from a
regular-file evidence identity. It accepts unrelated child churn in a safe
ancestor by comparing the opened and path snapshots on device, inode, owner,
group, and mode after applying the safety policy to both; inode replacement or
unsafe ownership/mode drift still fails. Regular files, claims, locks,
temporary publications, and durable directory snapshots retain their stricter
identities. The corrected directory-open boundary passed 82/82 allocator tests
and 20,000/20,000 churn-stress opens. The complete clean-source Lab suite result
is `4424 passed, 16 skipped, 26 warnings in 4975.41s (1:22:55)`. These are engineering checks, not immutable
source-bound campaign evidence.

The browser-egress source first attempted by v60, and retained by the current
Lab implementation, defines the mandatory boundary that must precede public
acquisition. Its current
contract contains 110 fresh-browser vectors: constructor, popup,
service-worker, URLLoader and browser-service negative probes; independent TCP,
UDP and DNS positive controls; and paired disabled/enabled controls for network
prediction, DNS prefetch, preconnect, speculation prefetch, speculation
prerender, Reporting and Network Error Logging. Production launches remain
ordinary Playwright Chromium launches and must validate the pinned driver,
executable, child wrapper, effective command line, managed policy and
fail-closed resolver before any browser context is created. Qualification-only
positive controls may change only their explicitly named treatment. Diagnostic
executions and unit tests do not satisfy this gate: the first eligible clean
post-repair cohort requires a newly clean immutable image and 110/110
packet-observed, checksum-closed results.
The frozen ordered expanded-vector digest is
`d9038cf12d733f914ad8e71365d98b8ac9eeb98aa9f02aa4ad43b992d3bd21ba`;
the standalone host watcher independently reconstructs that order and digest
instead of accepting an arbitrary set of 110 unique identifiers.
The checked-in
[`browser-egress-qualification-v1.json`](../../config/class-study/v1/browser-egress-qualification-v1.json)
manifest has SHA-256
`95d206391d283acd34c3393506730f9779eb09b06c467f2004ef78d619d72114`,
and its
[`browser-egress-chromium-argv-v1.json`](../../config/class-study/v1/browser-egress-chromium-argv-v1.json)
command-line contract has SHA-256
`458f51042d64433c089e5c43ab1167bbfa337ed4b6bda5e9d0d4efc0edf99c36`.
For the three trusted-anchor-ping vectors, successful activation plus suppression
under that pinned command line is the required semantic outcome; the harness
does not require a request that Chromium is explicitly configured not to send.
For legacy CSP reporting, the fixture applies the policy as an HTTP response
header in the exact page/frame document. Schema-4 denial evidence records the
exact URL and HTTP method, snapshots the pre-action stream, and validates only
the post-action suffix so unrelated earlier events cannot satisfy the vector.
The bounded wait is deliberately non-claiming: missing, duplicate, or wrong
denials remain visible to the semantic validator rather than becoming a
retriable browser-start failure.

Runner-wakeup schema 10 was the fail-closed scheduler evidence contract used by
v47. It retained schema 9's slot-owned
incoming identity inventory, strict release and no-catch-up policy, while
keeping ordinary output admission at two adapter windows before release and
moving the exact guard and active-wait tail to one actual adapter window: 5 ms
for aligned cells and 4.999 ms where adapter normalisation makes the strict
window fractional. On Linux AArch64, `CNTVCT_EL0` is predictive only: every
counter target is ceil-rounded, calibration brackets an authoritative
`Instant`, and a final authoritative `Instant` confirmation prevents early
dispatch. Every 64 successful relaxed counter reads now trigger an
authoritative `Instant` watchdog. The counter remains predictive only, and a
fresh authoritative transport check continues to enforce the same strict
half-open deadline. Other platforms use an authoritative-`Instant` fallback.

Schema 10 partitions every entered guard into `dispatch-ready` or one of five
typed terminal counter failures: invalid frequency, unavailable counter,
non-monotonic counter, frequency change, or target-calculation error. It binds
counter reads, calibrations, authoritative confirmations, early-confirmation
retries, exact watchdog cadence through the serialized
`buflo_exact_release_active_wait_authoritative_watchdog_cadence_validated_guards`
count, watchdog-selected dispatches, maximum
authoritative sample gaps, both directions of counter/authoritative divergence,
maximum counter gaps and calibration spans, plus separate bounded worst-success
and last-failure records. Fresh accepted BuFLO samples require every guard to
be dispatch-ready and every typed failure count to be zero.

The schema-10 validator also closes the receipt-integrity ambiguities
that a simple aggregate cadence check would leave. Every emitted integer
scalar and histogram count, and every present nested timestamp, is bound to
Rust's unsigned 64-bit domain. Structural count partitions, watchdog/read
products, and chronology
arithmetic use checked `u64` operations and reject overflow; saturation is not
an exemption from those identities. Where the Rust producer defines a
saturating aggregate, the nested-pair and residual checks apply that behaviour
explicitly. The schema-10-to-schema-9 compatibility projection removes the
additive watchdog fields and maps the production poll-source tag; among the
retained numeric identities, it normalises only cumulative wake totals and
histogram totals needed to reconstruct the frozen schema-9 relationships. It
does not weaken the other exact partitions.

For the retained worst successful guard, schema 10 requires
`active_duration = W + dispatch_lateness − guard_entry_lateness`, for exactly
one of `W ∈ {4,999,000, 5,000,000}` ns, with checked addition and no unsigned
underflow. The typed last-failure record now carries its exact iteration,
active-duration, interruption-count, interruption-duration, and maximum-gap
fields. Even when relative timestamps are nullable, it must satisfy, for the
same two possible windows, `X = guard_entry_lateness + active_duration`,
`exit_before_release = max(W − X, 0)`, and
`exit_at_or_after_deadline = (X ≥ 2W)`; when timestamps are present, the active
duration must also equal exit minus active-wait start. A sole success/failure
pair must reproduce the aggregate's saturating sums, maxima, and exact two-row
histogram. Mixed aggregates subtract the retained worst and, when present,
failure records, then constrain every hidden success by the remaining
cadence/read, interruption, counter/active-duration, and histogram budgets.
Histogram maxima
must occupy their claimed bucket with no occupied higher bucket, and the
interruption-duration floor couples the exact maximum with every additional
over-50-µs interruption. These checks prevent a valid nested record from
masking impossible residual guards.

The aggregate geometry is independently bounded rather than inferred only
from those residual counts. Aggregate entry lateness cannot exceed the larger
of the retained failure entry and `5,000,000 ns + maximum dispatch lateness`.
Aggregate active-wait time cannot exceed the exact retained success and
failure durations plus one `5,000,000 ns + maximum dispatch lateness` allowance
for each unretained success; when an otherwise-unretained success owns the
aggregate entry maximum, that entry lateness is subtracted before the one final
`u64` clamp. If the per-success addition itself overflows, the entry evidence
has lost the headroom required for safe subtraction, so the conservative
ceiling is `u64::MAX`. Active-derived aggregate maxima—spin gap, counter gap,
calibration span, authoritative sample gap, and authoritative counter lag—are
bounded by the exact retained per-metric maxima or one hidden-success active
ceiling, rather than by a retained guard's unrelated total duration. A
non-zero at-or-after-deadline dispatch count additionally requires maximum
dispatch lateness of at least 4,999,000 ns. Each successful nested guard binds
its actual adapter window to exactly 4,999,000 or 5,000,000 ns.

All schema-10 top-level and nested key sets are exact. Poll-source, phase, and
typed-failure outcome values must be canonical strings; JSON objects, arrays,
and other malformed enum values fail closed. Counter and relative-chronology
tuples are wholly present or wholly null as defined by their branch, the empty
guard projection is exact, and every early-confirmation retry requires
positive counter-observed progress. The Rust and Python schema-10 semantics
strings are byte-identical at 7,837 bytes with SHA-256
`fc86adeb78f468c2e01acc55324c561368664991d091e5ab86bb913374f7e0ae`;
the frozen schema-9 semantics remain
`6ab6713bde70c803a7f243432277edda4f6b850d732c9246f88413e61f487503`.

The 64-read rule is per guard, not the floor of one concatenated aggregate:
for guard `i`, `checks_i = floor(R_i/64)`, where `R_i` is that guard's
successful relaxed-read count after ordered calibration reads. The aggregate
therefore enforces `64×checks ≤ R < 64×(checks+guards)`, the per-guard residue
capacity, and the exact calibration/retry partition. Failure records retain
their own terminal-read and cadence allocation, and each early confirmation
retry requires a successful relaxed read. An authoritative-`Instant` fallback
must carry zero predictive/watchdog/divergence evidence. Zero BuFLO incoming
retry drives likewise requires zero maximum retry-wake lateness.

Release, the strict adapter-normalised half-open `[release, deadline)`
physical-realisation window (5 ms for aligned targets and 4.999 ms for
fractional targets), and the no-catch-up rule remain unchanged. Runner-wakeup
schemas 1–10 are historical, read-only compatibility inputs for their pinned
cohorts. None can admit a fresh BuFLO candidate cohort.

Runner-wakeup schema 11 retains the complete schema-10 layout for non-kernel
metrics and adds a typed `buflo_kernel_tx` receipt. Current source requires
nested kernel runner/item/mapping schema 2; nested schema 1 remains readable
only for its immutable historical lineage. Exact BuFLO egress is queued
with Linux `SO_TXTIME`/`SCM_TXTIME` against `CLOCK_TAI` and released through a
strict non-deadline ETF qdisc. One dedicated helper owns the timed socket
transaction; timed traffic uses the high-priority ETF band while ordinary
traffic remains on the FIFO band. Linux `TX_SCHED` and `TX_SOFTWARE` error-queue
timestamps, qdisc state and accounting, clock brackets, packet priority,
privilege drop, helper scheduling, exact job lifecycle, and all failure paths
are receipted fail closed. The former userspace exact-release guard metrics are
zero when kernel timing is active; they are retained only to preserve the
schema-10 compatibility envelope.

Nested schema 2 brackets `CLOCK_TAI` independently against
`CLOCK_MONOTONIC` and `CLOCK_REALTIME` at start, end, and after every retained
item. Every individual bracket and instant-alignment bracket remains limited to
250 microseconds. The `CLOCK_REALTIME` offset is a non-empty running
intersection and its final interval must remain within every provisional
interval. Each enqueue `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` timestamp is translated only through
that item's post-TX phase and must overlap the item's directly bracketed enqueue
TAI interval. The whole-run MONOTONIC envelope and exact start-relative maximum
drift are diagnostic because this WSL clock is periodically corrected; they do
not authorise a packet. Direct enqueue TAI-before-release, chronological clock
phases, physical transmit order, incoming `tx_lower >= enqueue_lower`, and the
strict half-open `[release, release + 5 ms)` observer-frame interval remain hard
gates.

The raw runner cannot establish observer-frame realisation by itself. The Lab
therefore captures independently at router ingress after the client veth and
before ingress netem, verifies start/end interfaces, routes, qdiscs, offloads,
forwarding and NAT state, reconciles every timed runner item in order, and
requires zero unresolved qdisc drops and zero capture drops. The resulting
`kernel-tx-evidence` sidecar is separately hash-bound while the accepted sample
retains its historical five-file inventory. The strict half-open
`[release, deadline)` window, outgoing-before-incoming logical order,
one-shot/no-catch-up policy, 1,200-byte target, ordinary HTTP/3 peer, and
client-only/non-paper-equivalent boundary remain unchanged.

opportunities per direction. A visit therefore contains 5,001–6,000 outgoing
opportunities, all of which—including `t=0`—must be kernel-timed and matched by
the independent post-veth observer. The separately reported `N−1`
post-`t=0` population is 5,000–5,999 per visit and 60,000–71,988 across all
twelve visits; it is the historical-comparison and sensitivity denominator,
not an omission of tick zero. Schema-11 visits reconcile every timed egress job
to Linux transmit feedback and the independent observer. Every suffix event is
subject to the same exact-size, strict half-open five-millisecond, no-catch-up,
credit, and terminal-drain checks as the mandatory prefix.

Logical outgoing-before-incoming ordering is established by each row's
direction, target, and slot identity. Physical `schedule.csv` order records
terminal resolution and may differ when an incoming credit resolves after a
later outgoing opportunity. Schema 4 therefore requires a complete contiguous
logical slot inventory without treating terminal-resolution row order as
dispatch order, plus the neutral schema-10 userspace projection, schema-11
sender receipt with nested kernel schema 2, Linux TX feedback, qdisc state,
router-ingress capture, and exact item reconciliation. Its `experiment.json`
checkpoint binds the cohort,
source, image, study/opportunity contract, parameters, network/environment,
workload, response qualification, and qualified chaff before any resume.
Timing-stress parameter/provenance, execution, and checkpoint schema 4 and

schema 5 are historical or reserved
and cannot authorise fresh evidence. The prelude remains one physical launch
per visit and is excluded from the unchanged 18-cell regression matrix and
every controlled or formal sample count. Any rejected, late, partial,
suppressed, missed, mismatched, catch-up, retired-credit, unresolved, dropped,
overlimit, requeued, or unmatched event fails the stress gate; retry cannot
convert it into a pass.

volume that actually backs Docker's data VHDX, rather than WSL's logical free
space. Authoritative `docker-desktop-data` registration and Docker settings
locations must agree when both exist. The launcher requires at least

`before-prepare`, `before-reference`, and `after-reference`; binds the probe,

daemon identity; and executes the exact source-controlled validator in
isolated Python mode. Within the current Unix user and WSL instance, a
daemon-ID-derived non-blocking lock is shared across checkouts and context
aliases. Fixed role tags, target-specific IID files, immediate and final
tag-to-ID checks, three distinct immutable image IDs, and cross-role source and
build-input comparisons make conflicting uncoordinated tag mutation fail
closed.

Fresh build-execution schema 5 retains schema 4's storage evidence, exact
IID-bearing command vectors, fixed tags, three distinct role IDs, per-role
source/build-input provenance, explicit
`docker --host <pinned-local-endpoint>` commands, and nested schema-1 `buildx`
envelope with policy
`docker-selected-buildx-binary-stability-v1`, selected through
`docker-info-client-plugin-metadata-v1` from Docker's exact
`.ClientInfo.Plugins` metadata (`Name`, `Path`, `SchemaVersion`,
`ShortDescription`, `Vendor`, and `Version`). The envelope records the reported lexical
plugin path and stat identity, optional symlink target, resolved root-owned
executable path and stat identity, resolved executable SHA-256, exact one-line
`docker buildx version` output, semantic version, and commit. Four strictly
increasing observations—`before-collection`, `after-collection`,
`after-prepare`, and `after-reference`—must all repeat the canonical identity
SHA-256 within the enclosing build interval. These are deliberately distinct
from the storage observations at `before-collection`, `before-prepare`,
`before-reference`, and `after-reference`. Any Buildx identity drift stops the
sequence before the next image build and prevents receipt creation. Schema 5
additionally binds the allocator's schema-1 cohort-allocation record, the
create-only claim and dense claim chain, and authority reproofs at the declared
build boundaries. The checked-in consumed ledger contains the dense prefix
1–61; receipt absence never makes one of those versions reusable, and the
allocator—not a hand-written version choice—is authoritative for the next claim.

A schema-5 build-execution receipt is deliberately not a successful build by
itself. Success is linearised only when the launcher publishes its canonical,
create-only sibling `build-completion-vN.json` at schema 1. That completion
binds the receipt's exact publication-time bytes and stat identity, source and
gitlink, cohort allocation/claim/chain, retired per-lease build transaction,
lifecycle/cohort/operation lock authority, and a final clean-source and claim
reproof immediately before publication. Current validators require both
siblings. Build-execution schemas 1–4 remain readable only behind an explicit
historical opt-in and cannot enter current admission.

Completion publication requires full stat identity. Later admission through a
Docker bind mount may change only volatile device, inode, UID, GID, mtime, and
ctime values: it still requires the same canonical relative path, schema,
cohort, bytes, payload and whole-file hashes, mode `0600`, link count one, and
exact size. The completion continues to project the immutable publication-time
stat rather than mount-local metadata. The stable reader also rejects a final
or component symlink, non-regular or multiply linked input, files larger than
16 MiB, unstable path/file identity, non-UTF-8 or duplicate-key/non-finite JSON,
and non-canonical content where required. These controls assume that the host
account owning the evidence tree is trusted. “Create-only” and “immutable” are
workflow and checksum properties, not protection against a malicious process
running later as that same UID; adversarial archival integrity requires an
independently protected copy or seal.

Current launcher-owned Docker API calls, attached and detached runs, and builds
also require a functioning per-user systemd manager on cgroup v2. Each Docker
client tree is placed in a unique transient service or scope with control-group
kill semantics; bounded API calls use a whole-cgroup runtime limit, and an
interrupted run or build cannot complete cleanup until the exact cgroup is
observed empty. Persistent user-owned lifecycle records bind the helper source,
boot, daemon, endpoint, context, process identity, scope/control group,
authenticated status channel, target container or network, and unpredictable
supervisor nonce. A stopped child remains behind durable birth and supervision
handshakes; source, latched signal, PID, start time, session, and process group
are revalidated immediately before release. Recovery validates every root
before mutation and orders run/build recovery before connected networks. An
authorised create whose object ID is still unavailable never expires merely
because a timeout elapsed: admission stays blocked until its uniquely labelled
object appears and can be authenticated and removed. No automatic daemon-reset
proof exists. A live owner, malformed or non-monotonic record, cross-daemon
state, unreadable `/proc` or cgroup state, ambiguous target, unresolved build
taint, or unprovably non-empty scope blocks execution. Environments without
functional user-systemd/cgroup-v2 containment are not evidentiary hosts and
fail closed. The receipts live beneath the private
`/var/tmp/qcsd-docker-lifecycle-<uid>/` namespace, while one private per-user
lifecycle lock serialises admission and recovery across checkouts. The
long-running acquisition watcher reaches that same admission boundary only
through an internal five-field bridge binding its state root, scope root,
request-authority digest, action digest, and source-binding digest; those
bindings are rechecked after the exclusive lifecycle lock is acquired and
immediately before stale Docker-state mutation. The acquisition watcher may
first contain only its own stale watcher scopes under its separate authority;
that pre-admission path cannot inspect or mutate Docker state.
The source-binding digest has explicit preimage-contract schema 2 and includes
the canonical build-completion path and SHA-256 alongside the build execution,
foundation, browser-egress, pinned-CDP, catalogue, provenance, source, and
preparation-image identities.

The lifecycle guardian treats private-session establishment and command
binding as distinct boundaries. After `setsid()`, it waits until the exact
pinned `/bin/bash --noprofile --norc -p ...` command is visible before periodic
runtime verification begins. A transient empty `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` during
`execve()` is therefore pre-admission construction; after exact binding,
command loss or drift remains fatal.

The guardian linearises each initial and final admission decision inside a
blocked-signal critical section. If a terminal signal is already visible for a
revalidated ready child, it is forwarded to the exact child process group once
and neither `GO` nor `ABORT` is published. The guardian instead retains the
decision-channel writer and all lifecycle authority until the child exits;
this prevents Bash from deferring its trap, consuming `ABORT`, and exiting 125
before the requested signal is handled. While an initial or final admission
channel is retained for signal denial, the guardian serves no new lifecycle
lease. An already admitted client may still obtain authenticated,
bounded cleanup leases after a later terminal signal, so ordinary Docker
teardown can complete during the grace period. A child that ignores the signal
is escalated to exact-group `SIGKILL` at the bounded cleanup deadline.
Non-signal admission failure continues to publish `ABORT`, and a signal that
arrives after the admission linearisation point follows the ordinary
post-admission forwarding path.
The native API-service watcher makes successful authority transfer definitive
before examining launcher termination; a short cleanup service that transfers
and exits in the same scheduler turn therefore follows post-transfer cgroup
terminal proof and propagates its actual status rather than a false `125`.

narrowly pinned ledger-only exception for the failed v57 teardown. It revalidated the
daemon, boot, source ancestry, and helper identity, proved the three run objects
and two network objects absent, and retired exactly their five stale `HANDOFF`
roots. It issued no Docker container, network, image, or volume removal; the
unrelated Docker inventory was unchanged. This closes the operational admission
blocker without turning recovery into scientific evidence or changing v57's
failed status.

The root page
recursively auto-attaches iframe, dedicated-worker, and shared-worker targets
with `waitForDebuggerOnStart`; every child acknowledges cache/service-worker
bypass, `Network`, recursive auto-attach, and an explicit target barrier before
it resumes. Dedicated and shared workers bind the exact first
`Debugger.scriptParsed` bootstrap and instrumentation pause, then install the
egress shim through `Debugger.evaluateOnCallFrame` before author code. This
prevents an early contextless `Runtime.evaluate` from publishing a structurally
valid but false receipt that classifies live `WebTransport` as unavailable.
Each worker actively proves that `WebTransport` resolves as a function, its
action is issued and rejected with `TypeError`, and exactly one
`paused-target-runtime-shim` telemetry notification is observed. For a held
runnable worker, post-ready `Debugger.scriptParsed` events are ignored without
retaining their identities, `functionLocation` line and column are exact
integers at `0:0`, and both initial and shutdown
`Runtime.runIfWaitingForDebugger` commands require exact empty-result
acknowledgements. For a held
runnable iframe, the paused-OOPIF pre-author barrier
arms `Debugger.setInstrumentationBreakpoint(beforeScriptExecution)`, installs
an exact-location conditional breakpoint at the first call frame, and uses its
false-returning condition to install the egress shim and popup guard and emit a
strict typed receipt before the first author statement executes. A no-script
frame is triggered only after both the resume acknowledgement and exact default
execution-context creation, using its `uniqueContextId`. The root page and
iframe sessions additionally enable request-stage
`Fetch`. Network events, not `Fetch` callbacks, create the retained
request-occurrence ledger. Fetch remains the before-I/O policy gate and its
sometimes page-owned worker interception must reconcile with the worker's
Network occurrence. Each dedicated/shared-worker fetch must also consume its
response body, emit the expected typed completion token, and reach a terminal
Network lifecycle; observing only JavaScript issue or
`Network.requestWillBeSent` is not completion. Session path, target ID, target generation,
request ID, and occurrence index form the frozen request identity. Redirects
may migrate across page/iframe sessions only when the active occurrence is
unique; target crashes, unsupported related targets, cache/service-worker
responses, ambiguous reuse, traffic emitted before setup acknowledgement,
unresolved setup/policy work at the deliberate shutdown boundary, and unmatched
terminal work fail closed. `./qcsd-lab test pinned-cdp` exercises the real pinned
Playwright/Chromium pair in the preparation image with Docker networking and
all capabilities disabled. No such receipt exists for v61 Lab code

checkpoint `18d4ab3144e04f0a012ffcba54d349b4046c4336`; v58 predates the mandatory

The probe constructs two distinct CDP sessions at their ownership boundary:
`context.new_cdp_session(page)` remains the root of
`RecursiveCdpTargetRouter`, while exactly one
`browser.new_browser_cdp_session()` is passed to `BrowserSharedWorkerGuard`

when installing the shared-worker guard even though narrower mocks did not
reach that boundary. The runtime regression
`test_probe_creates_browser_session_for_shared_worker_guard` now verifies
separate page/browser session routing, router-before-guard start order, and
browser closure on failure. This is source-level engineering evidence only.

is prerequisite evidence only; no passing browser-egress receipt or accepted
sample exists.

The separate browser-egress qualification exercises a fresh Chromium subject
for each of its 110 vectors on a fixed internal dual-stack fixture network. A
distinct observer shares only the browser network namespace, captures without a
filter, and must report zero kernel and interface drops. Negative vectors must
produce their exact semantic outcome while remaining silent at the forbidden
TCP, UDP and DNS sinks; independent positive controls must prove that each sink
and the packet observer can see the expected traffic. The browser runs as the
host-supplied non-root UID/GID with a read-only root, all capabilities dropped
and `no-new-privileges`; only the observer receives `CAP_NET_RAW`. Fixed
certificate, policy, executable, wrapper, topology, vector and expanded-vector
hashes are part of the immutable receipt. This is a browser
acquisition-surface gate, not a classifier sample and not evidence that advances
any campaign numerator.

All production and qualification preparation-browser profiles require one bare,
unique `--disable-quic`, reject `--enable-quic`, and load the sole managed policy
with `DnsOverHttpsMode=off`. This closes Chromium alternative-destination paths
at the frozen browser boundary. It does not change the Neqo capture transport:
fitting, certification, canary and formal replay remain ordinary HTTP/3 over
QUIC. The eventual live gate may therefore claim exact switch, policy, resolver
and packet-observation enforcement, but not a mechanism-specific Chromium HTTP/3
probe.

1.9 GB free. One controlled Docker Desktop restart remained in `starting` with
engine `_ping` timeouts, so Docker was stopped to avoid further writes. A
separate read-only probe on 1 September then resolved the registered and
configured Docker data VHDX to the same C: backing volume and observed only
about 2.19 GB free, well below the 64 GiB build floor. These observations are
operational context, not scientific evidence or observations in a successful
schema-2 receipt.

Later on 1 September, following explicit user authorisation, Docker's content
state was reset. Docker processes were stopped, `docker-desktop-data` and
`docker-desktop` were unregistered, and residual Local/Roaming Docker state
was removed. This permanently deleted the 612,543,496,192-byte data VHDX and
all containers, images, volumes, and build cache it contained. C: free space
rose to 614,881,771,520 bytes (about 572.65 GiB) after the reset and associated
cleanup. Docker Desktop application binaries and CLI configuration were
retained. At that reset checkpoint no Docker WSL distribution or data VHDX
was registered, and the next launch still had to initialise a blank engine.
Ubuntu-side cleanup also removed
four Rust `target` trees, regenerable caches, Linux trash, and 789 inactive
Codex session logs while preserving source, results, handoffs, receipts, and
sealed evidence. At that maintenance checkpoint no v34 build, campaign,
result, receipt, or `experiment.json` had been launched. Docker was later
reinitialised successfully and used for the immutable v34 attempt described
above.

<a id="source-project-md-l2420"></a>
### 1.3 Claim vocabulary

| Term | Meaning in this project |
|---|---|
| **Validated research defence** | One of the five previously integrated QCSD research defences with completed established-defence evidence. |
| **Candidate** | Implemented and testable, but not promoted because its dedicated validation attestation has neither been created nor independently verified. |
| **Baseline/control mode** | A selectable non-defence used for measurement or integration checks. `undefended` and `static` are not counted as research defences. |
| **Validated client-only QCSD adaptation** | The only permitted promotion description for BuFLO or CS-BuFLO after every hard gate passes. |
| **Paper-equivalent** | A prohibited claim for the live candidates. Both receipts set `implementation_scope=client_only_quic` and `paper_equivalent=false`. |

**Authoritative status string: “five validated defences plus two candidates / nine selectable modes”.**
In the thesis's Australian/British prose, the five validated research defences
are FRONT, Tamaraw, Traffic Morphing, WTF-PAD, and Walkie-Talkie. BuFLO and
CS-BuFLO are candidates; `undefended` and `static` are baseline/control modes.

The retained focused candidate workflow would create a versioned
`validation-attestation-vN.json`; the current 100-class endpoint instead
requires its own `validation-attestation.json`. Neither exists. Reports and
thesis text must not say
“seven validated defences” until the attestation is created by the fail-closed
verification path and independently verifies. Even after promotion, the claim
must remain “validated client-only QCSD adaptation”, never bilateral or
paper-equivalent.

The final class campaign must not be described as **ready** or **guaranteed**
until all 100 selected classes have passed acquisition and admission, every
applicable fitting and qualification gate has verified, and all 900 one-launch
class×mode certification cells have been accepted and independently verified.
Any earlier source, unit test, synthetic integration test, or partial campaign
is insufficient for that claim.

<a id="source-project-md-l2451"></a>
## 2. Research progression and principal outputs

<a id="source-project-md-l2453"></a>
### 2.1 Completed outputs

The established-defence work produced two reusable outputs:

- A sealed 120-sample undefended fitting campaign over six workloads, two
  request policies, and ten visits. It generated the deterministic
  `artifacts/research-1200/` (`artifacts/research-1200/`)
  Traffic Morphing, WTF-PAD, and Walkie-Talkie inputs.
- A separate 14-sample, seven-mode post-fit smoke over two workloads. All
  samples were accepted and eligible on their first attempts.

The later five-class acquisition produced the checksum-closed
`classifier-multiorigin5-v2` (`handoffs/classifier-multiorigin5-v2/`)
handoff:

| Property | Sealed value |
|---|---:|
| Acquisition results | 20 (baseline and paired results across ten temporal blocks) |
| Samples | 2,500 |
| Classes | 5 × 500 |
| Undefended | 1,500 |
| FRONT | 500 |
| Tamaraw | 500 |
| Training / validation / test / inference-only | 1,200 / 150 / 150 / 1,000 |
| `SHA256SUMS` entries | 12,503, all deep-verified again on 2026-09-04 |

This package is a classifier-pipeline pilot, not evidence of classifier or
defence efficacy. Its defined model-selection protocol trains only on
undefended blocks 1–8, validates on undefended block 9, tests on undefended
block 10, and treats FRONT and Tamaraw as inference-only. It is immutable and
is guarded before and after the candidate formal study.

<a id="source-project-md-l2628"></a>
## 3. Architecture, threat model, and evidence flow

<a id="source-project-md-l2630"></a>
### 3.1 Repository and execution boundaries

| Component | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| [`neqo-qcsd-lab/`](../..) | Host-side fail-closed cohort allocation and build admission, followed by Docker-isolated preparation, qualification, campaign execution, capture, verification, sealing, export, evaluation, and reporting. |
| [`neqo-qcsd-lab/neqo-qcsd/`](../../neqo-qcsd) | Rust QCSD controller, defence algorithms, QUIC/HTTP/3 integration, CLI, transport observations, and runner evidence. |
| Reference image | Network-disabled execution of pinned paper/author/archive inputs and the independent clean-room oracle. Author code is not copied into production Neqo. |
| Collection image | The clean client and Lab runtime used for live capture. It excludes author implementations. |
| Ordinary HTTP/3 servers | No defence extension, signalling protocol, padding-done message, or server configuration is required. |
| Handoff and evaluator | Create-only projections and hash-bound performance, correctness, and attack evaluation. |
| Final attestation | Reconstructs every hard gate and is the only promotion authority. |

The data and authority flow is:

```text
papers + pinned author source + CPSP archive
                    |
                    v
       isolated reference image ---> clean-room Lab oracle
                    |                         |
                    +------ conformance ------+

prepared workloads ---> Rust client-only QCSD ---> ordinary HTTP/3 servers
                              |                         |
                              +---- direct capture ----+
                                           |
                              five-file accepted sample
                                           |
Lab gates + frozen selection/assembly receipts + historical guard
                                           |
                         sealed raw/shape/CSV handoff
                                           |
                   evaluator + human comparison review
                                           |
                               final attestation
```

<a id="source-project-md-l2667"></a>
### 3.2 Scientific and observer scope

The live candidates are **client-only QUIC adaptations**. They control client
application sends, standard QUIC padding/chaff, and local receive-credit
attempts. They cannot schedule or prove a server's datagram size or emission
time. Incoming opportunities therefore mean locally scheduled credit/chaff
work followed by observed peer consumption; they are not synthetic claims
about server packets.

The completed historical classifier corpus uses five fixed, prepared,
multi-origin page-like replays. The prospective final study instead targets
100 prepared public-page classes drawn deterministically from a pinned Tranco
snapshot. Both are closed-world QUIC experiments, not reproductions of the
papers' 120-, 128-, or 200-site web populations and not open-world studies.
The final protocol is enforced by the checked-in class-study contract and
coordinator, while its still-unexecuted status is recorded in this ledger. The
observer frame is the direct Ethernet capture on the collection client's
interface. Classifiers receive only
relative timestamp, client-relative direction, and observer-frame length.
Address, port, connection ID, QUIC/TLS metadata, payload, URL, header,
controller, schedule, and defence labels are excluded.

These scope differences are not defects to normalise away. They must accompany
every original-study comparison, and an unexplained difference blocks the
attestation.

<a id="source-project-md-l2693"></a>
## 4. Nine-mode defence inventory

The selectable identity order is append-only. BuFLO and CS-BuFLO were appended
so all previous identities remain stable.

| Order | CLI/runtime identity | Research identity | Application participation | Parameter source | External input | Status |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `undefended` / `none` | Undefended baseline | No shaping | Resolved profile/controller baseline | None | Baseline, not a defence |
| 2 | `static` | Mechanical schedule control | Configurable `ChaffOnly` or `ChaffAndShape` | Signed `seconds,signed_size` CSV | Explicit schedule | Control, not a research defence |
| 3 | `front` | FRONT | `ChaffOnly` | Selected QCSD profile | None | Validated research defence |
| 4 | `tamaraw` | Tamaraw | `ChaffAndShape` | Selected QCSD profile | None | Validated research defence |
| 5 | `traffic-morphing` | Traffic Morphing | `ChaffOnly` reactive size shaping | Profile plus fitted matrix | Workload-bound matrix and workload ID | Validated research defence |
| 6 | `wtf-pad` | WTF-PAD | `ChaffOnly` | Profile plus fitted histograms | Histogram receipt | Validated research defence |
| 7 | `walkie-talkie` | Walkie-Talkie | `ChaffAndShape` | Profile plus fitted moulded bursts | Workload-bound mould and workload ID | Validated research defence |
| 8 | `buflo` | BuFLO | `ChaffAndShape` | Explicit versioned parameter JSON | `--buflo-parameters` | Candidate |
| 9 | `cs-buflo` | CS-BuFLO | `ChaffAndShape` | Explicit versioned CTSP or CPSP parameter JSON | `--cs-buflo-parameters` | Candidate |

The checked-in [`static-control-1200.csv`](../../config/defense-params/static-control-1200.csv)
is an integration control, not fitted efficacy evidence. The live JSON fixtures
for learned established defences are reviewed smoke inputs; formal fitting and
evaluation use the sealed research bundle.

<a id="source-project-md-l2715"></a>
## 5. Candidate implementation

<a id="source-project-md-l2717"></a>
### 5.1 Public interfaces and compatibility

The implementation adds `DefenseConfig::{Buflo(BufloConfig),
CsBuflo(CsBufloConfig)}` and `DefenseKind::{Buflo, CsBuflo}`. The CLI accepts
the `buflo` and `cs-buflo` modes and the `--buflo-parameters` and
`--cs-buflo-parameters` paths. Both implement
`DefenseMode::ChaffAndShape`.

Relevant source boundaries are:

- [`neqo-csdef/src/config.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-csdef/src/config.rs)
  for typed parameter parsing and fail-closed validation;
- [`neqo-csdef/src/profile.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-csdef/src/profile.rs)
  for stable runtime selection;
- [`buflo.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-csdef/src/defense/buflo.rs)
  and [`cs_buflo.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-csdef/src/defense/cs_buflo.rs)
  for the independent algorithms;
- [`neqo-bin/src/qcsd/mod.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-bin/src/qcsd/mod.rs)
  for client orchestration, exact release, endpoint selection, terminal drain,
  and `run.json` summaries;
- [`neqo-transport/src/connection/qcsd.rs`](../../neqo-qcsd/neqo-transport/src/connection/qcsd.rs)
  for packet-construction outcomes and composition evidence.

Resolved configuration remains schema 2 and accepts historical profiles and
results. All three candidate parameter JSONs use their independent schema 1;
the BuFLO provenance receipt is schema 1 and the two later CS-BuFLO provenance
receipts are schema 2. The historical CSV columns remain an exact prefix;
candidate fields are a versioned nullable suffix.

<a id="source-project-md-l2746"></a>
### 5.2 Canonical BuFLO adaptation

The canonical live receipt is
[`buflo-live.json`](../../config/defense-params/buflo-live.json).
It specifies a 20,000 µs cadence, an inclusive 10,000,000 µs minimum duration,
and exact 1,200-byte UDP-payload targets.

The implementation contract is:

- Both direction cursors begin at `t=0`. When times tie, the live client emits
  the outgoing opportunity before the incoming opportunity.
- Each tick uses application bytes when available and reviewed chaff or
  standard QUIC PING/PADDING otherwise. It never catches up a late tick by
  bursting multiple cells.
- The minimum-duration boundary is inclusive. After both next cursors pass
  `tau`, application completion closes replenishment. New opportunities stop
  only at a fresh whole-cell-capacity exhaustion boundary after required
  STREAM work, unconfirmed application sends, unadvertised credit, parser
  work, controller actions, and the due rolling identity are clear.
- Every outgoing cell must be exactly 1,200 observed UDP bytes. A partial,
  suppressed, missed, mismatched, late, or unresolved opportunity is a hard
  fidelity failure. Incoming non-success is likewise a hard failure.
- Let `W = deadline − release`, where `W` is 5 ms for aligned adapter windows
  and 4.999 ms for fractional windows. Ordinary-output admission is
  `release − 2W`; guard entry and active waiting begin at `release − W`; and
  the realisation window is the half-open interval `[release, release + W)`.
  Outgoing cells are prearmed, then guarded through the complete window.
  Incoming local credit is scheduled across the precise endpoint able to
  advertise it.
- For an exact outgoing/incoming pair, the outgoing socket handoff and its
  already-captured cross-endpoint credit handoff form one ordered transaction
  before global post-output reduction. Outgoing validity and reconciliation
  complete first; only the captured identity owner may receive one direct
  endpoint drive. The direct path retains the original absolute
  `[release, release + W)` boundary, fails closed on identity or committed-
  output conflicts, and does not alter catch-up, generic-output, or CS-BuFLO
  behaviour.
- Scheduled receive credit has separate advertisement and consumption
  boundaries. Local `MAX_STREAM_DATA` advertisement rearms cadence; later peer
  stream-offset consumption terminalises the opportunity. Requested,
  advertised, consumed, retired, and unresolved bytes must reconcile exactly.
- Schedule stop and final completion are distinct. Already-advertised,
  RTT-delayed `MAX_STREAM_DATA` is drain debt rather than authority to create a
  replacement cell. All outgoing cells are terminal at stop; incoming cells
  already advertised may terminalise later, and any retirement or miss still
  fails the run.
- A reviewed-chaff request may own scheduled incoming credit only after the
  peer has acknowledged its complete request bytes and FIN. Chaff send shaping
  remains active across schedule stop, so loss cannot revive targetless STREAM
  retransmission while the advertised response credit drains.
- At the terminal boundary, whole reviewed-chaff cells drain normally. A
  residual sub-cell tail can be cancelled only after scheduled work, parser
  leases, and application parser boundaries are clear; cancellation is typed
  as client-local HTTP/3 defence control. Final runner completion then requires
  application and chaff send halves to reach peer-confirmed `DataRecvd` or
  `ResetRecvd`, not merely local `DataSent`.

The canonical receipt allows 6,000 opportunities per direction: 12,000
combined opportunities and a 120-second cadence horizon. Generic validation
admits no more than 10,000 per direction (20,000 combined) and no more than a
120-second guard. These limits must not be described as a paper tuple. The
paper studied 1,000- and 1,500-byte TCP cells; the live 1,200-byte value is a
UDP-payload adaptation.

The receipted stop and tail policies are exactly
`stop_new_opportunities_at_first_terminal_whole_cell_capacity_exhaustion_then_drain_already_advertised_incoming_credit`
and
`drain_whole_cells_then_client_local_http3_cancel_unallocatable_reviewed_chaff_tail`.

Historical schemas 6–8 began the userspace active wait two actual adapter
windows before each 20 ms release—10 ms aligned or 9.998 ms fractional—and
could keep the client runnable for approximately 50% of one pinned CPU.
Schemas 9 and 10 retained ordinary-output admission two adapter windows before
target but moved the exact guard and active-wait tail to one adapter window
before target. Their expected active-wait cost was therefore approximately 25%
of one pinned CPU while canonical BuFLO was active. Those figures describe the
superseded userspace release path and remain useful historical wakeup evidence;
they are not a prediction for the current kernel-timed path.

Runner-wakeup schema 11 replaces userspace dispatch at release with a timed
socket transaction. The helper must enqueue the exact 1,200-byte datagram by
the release-minus-5-ms cutoff, attaches an absolute `CLOCK_TAI` transmit time
using Linux `SO_TXTIME`/`SCM_TXTIME`, and relies on strict non-deadline ETF for
the actual release. The high-priority timed ETF band is separate from ordinary
FIFO traffic. TX-scheduler and TX-software error-queue timestamps, the qdisc
and clock configuration, thread priorities, privilege drop, scheduler state,
and helper lifecycle are all receipted; the independent Lab observer then
reconciles the emitted datagram at router ingress. Legacy userspace guard and
active-wait fields remain only as a zero-valued compatibility projection in
kernel mode. Timer wakeups, helper CPU, finite CS retries, resolutions, and
deadline evidence remain measured performance data rather than infrastructure
noise.

The strict adapter-normalised half-open `[release, release + W)` window remains
5 ms for aligned cells and 4.999 ms for a receipted fractional window. Kernel
submission does not widen that window, permit catch-up, or weaken the rule that
every opportunity terminalises exactly once.

Incoming cell size and timing at the server remain unavailable properties.
The evidence proves only the client's scheduled credit/chaff attempt and the
ordinary peer traffic that is subsequently observed.

<a id="source-project-md-l2848"></a>
### 5.3 Canonical CS-BuFLO adaptations

The primary formal treatment uses
[`cs-buflo-ctsp-live.json`](../../config/defense-params/cs-buflo-ctsp-live.json).
The qualification-only ablation uses
[`cs-buflo-cpsp-live.json`](../../config/defense-params/cs-buflo-cpsp-live.json).
Both target 600-byte UDP payloads. The original code's 548-byte socket write
and nominal 600-byte IPv4/TCP packet are retained only as reference metadata;
they are not asserted to equal the live QUIC packet.

Shared canonical parameters are:

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---:|
| Initial interval `rho*` | 8,192 µs |
| Interval clamp | 4,096–32,768 µs |
| First adaptation boundary | 16,384 fresh real-bearing application bytes |
| Later boundaries | Double after each adaptation |
| Timing samples | At most 1,000 per direction |
| Jitter | `rho*k/100`, discrete `k` in 0–200 inclusive |
| Quiet interval | 2,000,000 µs |
| Event guard | 1,000,000 opportunities |
| Early termination | Client-local |

The rate estimator keeps direction-specific timing windows and inserts a
separator across direction changes. At each byte boundary it selects the upper
integer median eligible interval, floors it to a power of two, clamps it, and
retains the current rate when no paper-eligible sample exists. The live
adaptation counter is fresh, deduplicated application STREAM data: it excludes
retransmission, cover, QUIC padding, and defence control. This is an explicit
client-only design difference from the pinned prototype's transmitted
real-plus-junk counter.

The padding variants are:

| Variant | Outgoing frozen target | Incoming frozen target | Live progression and role |
|---|---|---|---|
| CTSP | Next power of two of realised total UDP payload | Payload target | Outgoing increments use observed UDP payload; incoming increments use fully consumed scheduled credit. Canonical formal treatment. |
| CPSP | Payload target | Payload target | The same directional increments; controlled qualification ablation. |

A payload target selects the ceiling power-of-two quantum from natural payload,
then the next multiple of that quantum covering natural plus defence-added
payload. CTSP's outgoing target instead uses the next power of two of its
realised total.

Application completion is the local `onLoad` analogue. The implementation
tracks directional powers-of-two and a strict quiet phase, freezes provisional
targets, and invalidates provisional stop evidence if natural traffic resumes.
Local early termination stops further defence chaff and credit work, then
drains already-advertised receive credit exactly once before latching. It does
not stop ordinary application progress; pre-onLoad receive streams, parser
state, and send endpoints are handed back to ordinary HTTP/3 processing.

Outgoing opportunities are congestion-sensitive and one-shot. The transport
may realise a full cell, a smaller exact datagram when congestion permits only
part, or suppress it when pacing/congestion permits no suitable defence
packet. Partial and suppressed outcomes require typed transport proof and are
never queued for catch-up. Actual misses or unresolved slots remain failures.

Incoming opportunities are stricter: scheduled credit is assigned, locally
advertised, then eventually consumed by an observed peer stream offset. A
partial, suppressed, missed, retired, or unresolved incoming opportunity is a
hard failure. After the immediate post-action output turn, an accepted but
unencoded credit identity may receive at most three owning-endpoint output
turns at one-quarter, one-half, and three-quarters of its existing 5 ms
half-open window. These are finite transport retries, not scheduler catch-up:
they cannot run before the target or at/after the deadline, and the deadline
still expires irreversibly. Neither this split lifecycle nor local early
termination is the paper's bilateral server padding-complete signal.

<a id="source-project-md-l2918"></a>
### 5.4 Shared action and evidence boundary

The Rust boundary distinguishes `QcsdSendPolicy::Exact` from
`QcsdSendPolicy::CongestionSensitive`. Terminal event outcomes are
`Satisfied`, `FullySatisfied`, `PartiallySatisfied`, `Suppressed`, or `Missed`.
Only CS-BuFLO egress may accept partial/suppressed outcomes, and only when the
transport supplies a pacing/CWND-limited reason. BuFLO and every candidate
ingress opportunity require full satisfaction.

Typed failure/limitation reasons cover pacing and congestion window, key
unavailability, path MTU, mandatory frames, endpoint closure, retired credit,
timer lateness, and deadline expiry. Candidate runs publish both an aggregate
egress-backlog edge and a split required-STREAM/unconfirmed-application edge.
This lets CS-BuFLO retain aggregate quiet/final-drain semantics while BuFLO
distinguishes real send work from already-encoded receive-control debt; a
monotonic observation barrier prevents stale snapshots from authorising stop.
Terminal client-local chaff cancellation invalidates the snapshot that
authorised it; `DefenseComplete` remains blocked until the runner recomputes
and reduces a fresh, actual aggregate empty snapshot after every cancellation
action has crossed into the adapter.

Packet-build evidence separates desired and observed UDP bytes, application
STREAM data, retransmission STREAM data, reviewed chaff, defence control, QUIC
padding, other QUIC bytes, datagram class, congestion reason, and lateness.
Scheduled-credit evidence separately records requested, advertised, consumed,
retired, and unresolved amounts.

The accepted-sample CSV readers preserve the historical columns as an exact
prefix and accept the versioned nullable suffix in `schedule.csv`,
`events.csv`, and `packets.csv`. Packet-composition suffix data is schema 2.
The final nullable suffix field, `terminal_defense_elapsed_us`, is populated
only on current `schedule.csv` terminal rows, whose outcome schema is 3. It is
the controller's exact terminal-resolution offset from defence start, floored
to microseconds; the same field remains blank in `events.csv` and
`packets.csv`. `action_time_us` remains the first adapter action, while
`credit_consumed_at_us` remains a process-clock observation translated through
the receipted defence-start anchor before comparison. Ordinary terminal
resolutions cannot predate their target. Typed future-slot cancellation exists
only for failed-attempt evidence, whose missed outcome cannot enter an accepted
candidate sample.

All authoritative runner-CSV consumers now share one canonical unsigned parser
matching Rust's `u64` text domain. It accepts `0` through
`18446744073709551615` as canonical ASCII decimal and rejects signs,
whitespace, non-ASCII digits, fractional notation, leading zeroes other than
the single value `0`, overlong text, and numeric overflow before any timing,
size, composition, credit, fitting, plotting, or handoff arithmetic occurs.
The schema-4 timing-stress validator additionally requires the exact current
`schedule.csv` prefix-plus-nullable-suffix header in its defined order; an
omitted, inserted, duplicated, or reordered column cannot be admitted as
current evidence.

`run.json` contains BuFLO summary schema 4, CS-BuFLO summary schema 4, and,
for a fresh BuFLO run, runner-wakeup schema 11 with nested kernel runner/item/
mapping schema 2 alongside resolved
configuration, hashes, monotonic anchors, endpoints, application responses,
and chaff responses. BuFLO schema 4 binds the stop policy, stop time, residual
whole-cell capacity, scheduled and terminal direction counts at stop, and the
subsequent exact credit drain; its nested `buflo_state` is schema 3.
Historical BuFLO summary schemas 2 and 3 remain read-only compatible.

Historical runner-wakeup schemas 1–10 remain readable, but cannot admit fresh
candidate evidence. Schema 7 retains schema 6's complete unrealised slot-owned
identity inventory with same-tick refresh, exact-slot expiry, and coincident
admission, guard, and active-wait entry two actual adapter windows before
release (10 ms aligned or 9.998 ms fractional). It adds one
per-guard dispatch-lateness sample, one maximum active-gap sample, a bounded
worst-guard record, and Linux monotonic-raw/thread-CPU auxiliary-clock evidence.
Schema 8 preserves that contract while removing the architecture-specific spin
hint from its immediate-`Instant` poll. Schema 9 separates ordinary admission
from the one-window exact guard, replaces critical-path auxiliary-clock sampling
with predictive-counter and authoritative-confirmation evidence, and records a
complete dispatch-ready/typed-failure partition with bounded success and failure
chronologies. Counter evidence never authorises dispatch without a final
authoritative `Instant` confirmation.
Schema 10 preserves schema 9's geometry and partition, samples authoritative
`Instant` every 64 successful relaxed counter reads, and adds bounded
watchdog-selection and directional clock-divergence evidence.
Schema 11 retains the schema-10 layout for non-kernel metrics and adds the
nested `buflo_kernel_tx` receipt. In kernel mode the superseded userspace
exact-release guard metrics are zero. Raw sender evidence in `run.json` is not
sufficient by itself: the Lab independently observes post-client-veth,
pre-netem router ingress, verifies the interfaces, routes, qdiscs, offloads,
NAT, capture bounds, and qdisc end state, and requires item-by-item reconciliation
with zero unresolved qdisc or capture drops.

Accepted current-study samples retain separately hash-bound scheduler-runtime
and `kernel-tx-evidence` sidecars inside `experiment.json`, preserving the
exact five-file sample inventory. Pre-window, already-advertised unowned parser
capacity still cannot satisfy a new exact opportunity.

<a id="source-project-md-l3032"></a>
### 6.2 Oracle contract

The BuFLO oracle covers all eight published `(tau, rho, d)` configurations. It
includes the `t=0` tick, 52-byte TCP/IP accounting, exclusion of pure 52-byte
ACKs, inclusive `timer <= tau`, and continuation after `tau` only while source
or real buffers remain. The pinned author simulator processes incoming before
outgoing on a tied tick; the live QCSD adaptation processes outgoing before
incoming. The paper models independent bidirectional endpoints and does not
specify this tie order. Source-order and live-order projections are therefore
both hashed, while all eight source-compatible traces match author output.

The CS-BuFLO oracle covers the estimator, direction separators, 1,000-sample
limit, median/power-of-two/clamp rule, jitter grid, total/payload padding,
quiet-state transitions, padding-done state, and early termination. The
released CPSP audit includes 3,824 non-zero-baseline records out of 4,000 and
reproduces:

```text
7,592,598,380 / 3,326,013,453 = 2.282792444255336
```

<a id="source-project-md-l3053"></a>
### 6.3 Published BuFLO anchors

BuFLO's table reports **additional bandwidth overhead**, not a multiplicative
ratio. Latency is an absolute number of added seconds.

| `(tau ms, rho ms, d bytes)` | Extra BW | Added latency | Panchenko | VNG++ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `(0,40,1000)` | 93.5% | 6.0 s | 27.3±1.8% | 22.0±2.1% |
| `(0,40,1500)` | 120.0% | 3.6 s | 23.3±3.3% | 18.3±1.0% |
| `(0,20,1000)` | 140.5% | 2.4 s | 20.9±1.6% | 15.6±1.2% |
| `(0,20,1500)` | 201.3% | 1.2 s | 24.1±1.8% | 18.4±0.9% |
| `(10000,40,1000)` | 129.2% | 6.0 s | 14.1±0.9% | 12.5±0.8% |
| `(10000,40,1500)` | 197.5% | 3.6 s | 9.4±1.3% | 8.2±0.8% |
| `(10000,20,1000)` | 364.5% | 2.4 s | 7.3±1.0% | 5.9±1.0% |
| `(10000,20,1500)` | 418.8% | 1.2 s | 5.1±0.7% | 4.1±0.8% |
